# Codex verification brief — relation map on the try-it page (commit bfcb83d)

You are an independent reviewer. Do NOT edit any tracked file. Write scratch files only under
`audit_evidence_claims/m1/`. Put your report in `audit_evidence_claims/m1/REPORT.md`.
End with exactly one line: `VERDICT: APPROVE` or `VERDICT: CHANGES REQUIRED`.

## What was added

`src/app.py` `Engine.explore(text, per_group=30)` and `POST /api/explore`; `web/try.html` section "0. 관계 지도"
(`drawMap`, `explore`, `itemDetail`, the GKO/TKO/ACT/PH tables, click-through and the "따라온 길" trail);
`tests/test_app.py::test_explore_draws_a_drug_and_refuses_a_biosimilar`. Run tests with
`python -m pytest tests -q -p no:cacheprovider` (317). Start the page with `python src/app.py [port]` (use a port
other than 8765, which may already be in use) and exercise it over HTTP.

The page is for a domain reviewer to look at one node (drug, target, company, investigator, trial, disease) and
everything a live edge ties it to, grouped by relation, and to click through. It is read-only.

## The guarantees claimed (page text in section 0 and the `explore` docstring)

1. Every neighbour shown is joined to the centre by a live (not retired) edge to a live node, and the group label
   states the relation and its direction correctly (e.g. "이 약의 임상을 주관한 곳 (주관 ≠ 개발)" for
   Company -SPONSORS_TRIAL_OF-> Drug seen from the drug).
2. Details shown for a neighbour are the stored values: trial phases/status/start/title/url, sponsor trial count
   (`evidence_trials`) and sponsor class, drug trial count in the graph (EVALUATES), max phase, action type.
3. A name the question linker refuses (biosimilar, distinct product, tracer) is reported as refused, never drawn
   as the originator molecule; an unknown name says "not found".
4. Groups are ordered by size and each list keeps the `per_group` neighbours with most evidence; the page says
   when a list is cut and shows the true total.
5. Nothing is written; no model is called.

## What to verify (reproduce; cite file:line and exact inputs)

A. **Wrong relations or directions.** Any centre where an edge is grouped under the wrong label or direction,
   self-loops (src == dst), edges with both ends equal to the centre, PARENT_OF/SPONSORS/STUDIES/ASSOCIATED_WITH
   direction wording, Investigator vs Company handling, nodes whose type the tables do not cover.
B. **Name resolution.** `explore` takes the first non-refused hit of `link_entities`. Inputs with several entities
   ("osimertinib EGFR"), a refused token next to a real one ("Mvasi bevacizumab"), salts vs parents, company aliases
   with `also_matching_ids` (only the first id is drawn — is that misleading?), node ids vs names that collide,
   case/whitespace/Unicode, long inputs. Is anything drawn that the input does not name, or a refused product drawn
   as the originator?
C. **Wrong values.** Trial counts, sponsor counts, max phase and action shown versus the database
   (`data/processed/oncokg.sqlite`); retired edges/nodes leaking in; counts computed over retired edges;
   the `trials` count chunking; JSON props that are null or malformed.
D. **Cutting and ordering.** Is the "most evidence" ordering what the page says (trials are ordered by start date
   string — is that what the text implies)? Does the map's subset (`drawMap` allocation) ever hide a whole group
   or misstate totals? The Disease node (thousands of neighbours) — performance and output size.
E. **Page safety and correctness.** HTML/SVG injection from labels, titles, ids, URLs (`href` with `javascript:`?),
   edge ids in `data-id` attributes, the trail/back buttons after errors, double clicks, refused/not-found states,
   dark mode legibility is not in scope.
F. **Server.** Error handling for empty/oversized input, concurrency with the lock, behaviour when the DB has
   retired rows.
G. **Docs and wording.** Section 0 text, README/README.ko lines for `app.py`, the module docstring: do they claim
   more than the code does? Korean labels in GKO/ACT/PH that misstate the stored code.

## Approval bar

No known way for the map to show a relation that is not a live edge, a wrong direction or relation wording, a
value that differs from the stored one, a refused product drawn as the originator, or script injection. Report
should-fix items (misleading wording, cut lists without notice) separately from blockers.

---
name: review
description: Review the changes since a fixed point (commit, branch, tag, or merge-base) along two axes ??Standards (does the code follow this repo's documented coding standards?) and Spec (does the code match what the originating issue/PRD asked for?). Runs both reviews in parallel sub-agents and reports them side by side. Use when the user wants to review a branch, a PR, work-in-progress changes, or asks to "review since X".
---

Two-axis review of the diff between `HEAD` and a fixed point the user supplies:

- **Standards** ??does the code conform to this repo's documented coding standards?
- **Spec** ??does the code faithfully implement the originating issue / PRD / spec?

Both axes run as **parallel sub-agents** so they don't pollute each other's context, then this skill aggregates their findings.

The issue tracker should have been provided to you ??run `/setup-matt-pocock-skills` if `docs/agents/issue-tracker.md` is missing.

## Process

### 1. Pin the fixed point

Whatever the user said is the fixed point ??a commit SHA, branch name, tag, `main`, `HEAD~5`, etc. If they didn't specify one, ask for it.

Capture the diff command once: `git diff <fixed-point>...HEAD` (three-dot, so the comparison is against the merge-base). Also note the list of commits via `git log <fixed-point>..HEAD --oneline`.

Before going further, confirm the fixed point resolves (`git rev-parse <fixed-point>`) and the diff is non-empty. A bad ref or empty diff should fail here ??not inside two parallel sub-agents.

### 2. Identify the spec source

Look for the originating spec, in this order:

1. Issue references in the commit messages (`#123`, `Closes #45`, GitLab `!67`, etc.) ??fetch via the workflow in `docs/agents/issue-tracker.md`.
2. A path the user passed as an argument.
3. A PRD/spec file under `docs/`, `specs/`, or `.scratch/` matching the branch name or feature.
4. If nothing is found, ask the user where the spec is. If they say there isn't one, the **Spec** sub-agent will skip and report "no spec available".

### 3. Identify the standards sources

Anything in the repo that documents how code should be written, such as `CODING_STANDARDS.md` or `CONTRIBUTING.md`.

### 4. Spawn both sub-agents in parallel

Send a single message with two `Agent` tool calls. Use the `general-purpose` subagent for both.

**Standards sub-agent prompt** ??include:

- The full diff command and commit list.
- The list of standards-source files you found in step 3.
- The brief: "Report ??per file/hunk where relevant ??every place the diff violates a documented standard. Cite the standard (file + the rule). Distinguish hard violations from judgement calls. Skip anything tooling enforces. Under 400 words."

**Spec sub-agent prompt** ??include:

- The diff command and commit list.
- The path or fetched contents of the spec.
- The brief: "Report: (a) requirements the spec asked for that are missing or partial; (b) behaviour in the diff that wasn't asked for (scope creep); (c) requirements that look implemented but where the implementation looks wrong. Quote the spec line for each finding. Under 400 words."

If the spec is missing, skip the Spec sub-agent and note this in the final report.

### 5. Aggregate

Present the two reports under `## Standards` and `## Spec` headings, verbatim or lightly cleaned. Do **not** merge or rerank findings ??the two axes are deliberately separate (see _Why two axes_).

End with a one-line summary: total findings per axis, and the worst issue _within each axis_ (if any). Don't pick a single winner across axes ??that's the reranking the separation exists to prevent.

## Why two axes

A change can pass one axis and fail the other:

- Code that follows every standard but implements the wrong thing ??**Standards pass, Spec fail.**
- Code that does exactly what the issue asked but breaks the project's conventions ??**Spec pass, Standards fail.**

Reporting them separately stops one axis from masking the other.

| B2 | a collaborator was labelled the trial's lead sponsor | SPONSORS groups keep `role`; headings say lead sponsor or collaborator |

| count | drug trial counts included retired trials | counts join live Trial nodes |
| should-fix | ordering wording, ring cut silently, stale trail after errors, out-of-order replies, ambiguous names silent, action/class labels, omitted details, malformed props, `javascript:` URLs, "All three" | each group states its actual sort; ring says drawn/total; trail cleared on refusal/not-found/error; replies older than the latest request are dropped; the reading and other matches (company, target, refused span) are shown; stored action codes shown with a Korean gloss; sponsor class code shown; centre start date, drug count and phase always shown; props that are not objects read as empty; only clinicaltrials.gov links are clickable; docstring fixed |

## Round 4 (audit_evidence_claims/m4) — CHANGES REQUIRED

Shortest-span binding kept failing (wrong drug on ties, a six-token reach, qualifiers already consumed by the token pass).
Replaced by a fail-closed rule: text is cut into list items at , ; ， ； 、 and listing words; an item in which any run of
words names a distinct product or tracer has every drug in it refused (which drug the qualifier describes is not guessed);
drugs in other items, and targets/companies in the same item, still link. M4-B1 (both drugs now refused, never
BEVACIZUMAB), M4-B2 (no distance cap), M4-B3 (one pass, no pre-consumed qualifier) and the EGFR/AstraZeneca over-refusal
are covered by tests. Tests 326.

## Round 5 (audit_evidence_claims/m5) — CHANGES REQUIRED

| --- | --- | --- |
| M5-B1 | a glued "/" or "|" ("bevacizumab-awwb/ osimertinib") hid the suffix guard; the alias key dropped the slash and linked BEVACIZUMAB | tokens are stripped of "/" and "|", which end the list item |
| M5-B2 | "liposomal and nanoparticle formulation of doxorubicin": "and" detached the bare qualifier | a qualifier item with no drug carries across a listing word to the next item's drugs; a product named by itself (in the index, or a known biosimilar/reformulation brand such as Mvasi, Abraxane) does not carry |
| M5-S1 | names were built across list items ("biosimilar anastrozole, Arimidex" lost Arimidex; "Erlotinib, hydrochloride ..." became the salt) | drug spans and the positive matcher stay inside one item |
| M5-S3 | the all-runs scan was cubic (1.8 s at 250 words, under the lock) | whole item once for unanchored patterns, runs of at most eight words for anchored ones: 500 words in about 0.03 s |
| M5-S2 | "Pegylated interferon alfa-2a" (a stored alias of the pegylated drug itself) is refused | not changed: the resolver refuses it too, and exempting known aliases from the product guards would reopen M5-B1 (recorded as a known over-refusal) |

# Independent relation-map review — round 8

Reviewed **`ec4eede`**, 2026-11-03 KST, against the supplied m8 design and the unchanged approval bar. **Approval is blocked by three reproduced originator-leak mechanisms.** The exact named m7 blockers are repaired, but `[89 Zr] trastuzumab` is a new regression; internal suffix wrappers and omitted Unicode dashes still evade the three-stage pipeline. Passing graph checks and demos do not establish that the selected identity is safe.

All new artifacts are under `audit_evidence_claims/m8/`; no tracked file was changed. Known **M5-S2** and explicitly intended same-item over-refusal are accepted. Loss of a valid drug alone in its item is reported separately, as requested.

## Verification and reproduction boundaries

## Blocking findings

### M8-B1 — P1, new regression: the resolver refuses a tracer, but its refusal is lost

Exact request: `POST /api/explore` with `{"text":"[89 Zr] trastuzumab"}`.

Actual HTTP 200 draws **TRASTUZUMAB / CHEMBL1201585**, **13 trials**, `also:[]`. There is no separately accepted originator mention.

| Stage/control | Observed result |
|---|---|
| `/api/resolve` | `BLOCKED_CLASS_TERM`, `radiotracer_guard`, no drug ID; cleaned component is `trastuzumab` |
| `_resolver_blocked` after input normalization | `{}` |
| `_link` | Drug / CHEMBL1201585 |
| `_whole_name` | `None`; the unsafe item-linker result survives |
| Exact previous query module, `9bd7cb4` | Refused; no centre |
| `[89Zr] trastuzumab`, `89Zr trastuzumab` | Correctly refused |
| Bare `trastuzumab` | Correctly draws the unlabelled drug |

`Which trials evaluate [89 Zr] trastuzumab?` returns **12 originator evidence rows** over HTTP, first `NCT00016367|EVALUATES|CHEMBL1201585|NCT00016367`. The bracket-without-space control returns zero. Sponsor QA independently returns 12 rows in the direct engine probe. Tab, newline and NBSP between the isotope mass and element reproduce the same new regression. [HTTP](root_checks/new_http.json), [exact predecessor/stages](attacks/probe_results.json), [detailed analysis](attacks/FINDINGS.md).

**Cause:** `src/query.py:187-190` re-tests the cleaned component instead of honoring the resolver's recorded `radiotracer_guard`. The resolver correctly checks the raw input (`src/normalize.py:738-746`), but its component has already lost the bracketed isotope. `_link` now strips the brackets from separate words (`src/query.py:239-242`), creating `89 Zr trastuzumab`. `_qualified` checks isotope labels only one word at a time (`112`); its multiword scan does not call `is_isotope_label` (`114-116`), although that predicate supports `89 Zr` (`src/textrules.py:289-298`). Aggressive alias matching still succeeds (`src/query.py:317-322`; `src/textrules.py:339-346`). The final whole-name tracer guard recognizes the original tracer and returns `None` (`src/query.py:211-212`), but that is treated only as absence of a whole-name match: it supplies no refusal and the caller returns the unsafe `found` result (`149-153`).

**Required outcome:** preserve the explicit tracer refusal and its affected molecule through cleaning and tokenization; this exact input must never display or answer from the unlabelled drug.

### M8-B2 — P1, inherited: internal suffix wrappers defeat every refusal stage

**`bevacizumab-(awwb)`** and **`bevacizumab-[awwb]`** each return HTTP 200, **BEVACIZUMAB / CHEMBL1201583**, **122 trials**, no refusal. Both also leaked in the exact predecessor. Controls `bevacizumab-awwb`, `(bevacizumab-awwb)` and spaced `bevacizumab - (awwb)` refuse.

The resolver accepts the plain molecule after parenthetical cleaning; `_resolver_blocked` is empty. The item linker leaves an interior opening bracket/parenthesis, defeating the anchored suffix guard, then removes the distinction in `canonical_key`. `_whole_name` returns `None`, leaving the leaked Drug. `awwb` is a suffix explicitly classified by the repository itself (`src/textrules.py:169-171`); no external pharmacology assumption is needed.

`Which trials evaluate bevacizumab-(awwb)?` returns **12** originator rows over HTTP, first `NCT00955305|EVALUATES|CHEMBL1201583|NCT00955305`. `bevacizumab-(awwb), osimertinib` still makes BEVACIZUMAB the centre and lists OSIMERTINIB as an alternative; the separate drug does not authorize the originator leak. [HTTP](root_checks/new_http.json), [stages and analogous trastuzumab cases](attacks/probe_results.json).

**Cause:** edge-only wrapper removal at `src/query.py:239-242`, anchored guard at `src/textrules.py:156-157`, followed by broader positive canonicalization at `src/textrules.py:339-346` / `src/query.py:320-322`. Resolver cleaning is at `src/textrules.py:358-363`.

**Required outcome:** internal wrapper variants of a refused product must not gain originator identity through the lookup key.

### M8-B3 — P1, inherited: the advertised universal dash fold omits actual dash characters

**`bevacizumab⸺awwb`**, with **U+2E3A TWO-EM DASH**, returns HTTP 200, **BEVACIZUMAB / CHEMBL1201583**, **122 trials**, no refusal. Trial QA returns the same 12 originator rows. ASCII-hyphen and U+2011 controls refuse. The exact previous query module also leaks U+2E3A.

NFKC preserves this character. `_DASHES` at `src/query.py:100-101` excludes it; `_resolver_blocked` returns `{}`, `_link` accepts the aggressive alias key, and `_whole_name` returns `None`. The key erases the dash at `src/textrules.py:345`. A bounded scan of this Python runtime's **26 Pd code points** records **14 originator leaks**, including U+2E3B and U+2E40. This is an exhaustive Pd scan, not a claim to cover every dash-like Unicode symbol. [Inventory](attacks/unicode_dashes.json), [HTTP](root_checks/new_http.json), [stages](attacks/probe_results.json).

**Required outcome:** the normalization/refusal representation must cover the dash equivalences accepted by positive lookup. The source's “every Unicode dash” claim currently exceeds its implementation.

## Should-fix findings, separate from the originator approval blockers

### M8-S1 — P2, new: cache rollover rejects a valid lone drug

On a fresh original CLI server, send **82 successful `/api/explore` requests** containing the 4,096 distinct pieces `isa x0000` through `isa x4095`, in semicolon batches of at most 50 (each request at most **499 characters**). Then send `{"text":"osimertinib"}`:

- First request: **HTTP 400**, `{"error":"'osimertinib'"}`.
- Immediate identical retry: **HTTP 200**, **CHEMBL3353410**, **79 trials**.

No cache was injected or monkey-patched. The original inherited 7,511-key sweep independently hit the same exception at `Gallium gozetotide ga-68`; its failure transcript is retained. [Natural HTTP reproduction and exact batches](demos_cost/cache_boundary_http.json), [reproducer](demos_cost/cache_boundary_http.py), [historical replay/adaptation evidence](replays/FINDINGS.md).

`src/query.py:174-182` inserts entry 4,097, clears the entire cache at `180-181`, then immediately indexes the removed key at `182`. The HTTP wrapper turns that `KeyError` into a 400 (`src/app.py:267-269`). Keep the current resolution available across eviction. This is a new valid-input reliability defect, not accepted over-refusal or a permanent deadlock.

### M8-S2 — P2, inherited: qualifier carry crosses an explicit item boundary

`biosimilar, and osimertinib`, `biosimilar; and osimertinib`, and `biosimilar and, osimertinib` all return only Refused `biosimilar ... osimertinib`. Their controls `biosimilar, osimertinib` and bare `osimertinib` draw **CHEMBL3353410 / 79 trials**. HTTP QA for the first form yields zero evidence; the comma-only control yields 12.

The valid drug is alone in its punctuation-delimited item, so this falls outside the requested intended-over-refusal allowance. The input was already present in m7's supplemental attack; it is **NOT FIXED**, not newly introduced. `joined` checks only intervening joiner segments, ignoring `clause` boundaries (`src/query.py:282-283`), then carries the qualifier at `298-304`. [HTTP](root_checks/new_http.json), [m7-script replay](m7_attacks/fresh_results.json).

### M8-S3 — P2, new: isotope-shaped stored aliases lose valid lone-name recognition

| Exact stored alias | Stored Drug ID | Current map | Exact m7 query module |
|---|---|---|---|
| `566C` | ATOVAQUONE / CHEMBL1450 | Refused | Correct Drug |
| `9-ac` | 9-AMINOCAMPTOTHECIN / CHEMBL274070 | Refused | Correct Drug |
| `5ac` | AZACITIDINE / CHEMBL1489 | Refused | Correct Drug |

All three are exact unique dictionary aliases, supplied alone. Preferred-name controls still work. The resolver also refuses these as isotope labels; this is **shared over-refusal**, not resolver disagreement. The new unconditional one-token isotope test (`src/query.py:112`) prevents whole-name recovery (`208-209`). These additional lone-name losses are separate from explicitly accepted M5-S2. Evidence establishes the repository's stored identities; no clinical suitability claim is made. [Dictionary/stages/predecessor](attacks/lone_alias_results.json), [actual HTTP](alias_checks/new_http.json).

### M8-S4 — P2, new: a literal terminal period in a stored name is deleted before exact comparison

**`5.BETA.,20-EPOXY-1,2.ALPHA.,4,7.BETA.`** is an exact unique stored PACLITAXEL alias, **CHEMBL428647**. Current HTTP returns **not_found**; the exact previous query module draws PACLITAXEL with **234 trials**, and current preferred-name/ID controls still work. No current stage refused this input.

`src/query.py:210` strips the name's required terminal period, but `215-216` compares against the unstripped stored alias. The input really is letter-for-letter the stored name before that asymmetric operation. Preserve literal stored punctuation when applying the stated exact-name exception, without weakening product refusals. [Stored-name/predecessor evidence](attacks/lone_alias_results.json), [HTTP](alias_checks/new_http.json).

## Cost and demos

M7-S3's **exact repeated non-drug piece** defect is **FIXED**. A final comparison ran after the other audit workloads finished; unrelated host activity remains uncontrolled. It loads exact `9bd7cb4` and current query modules against the same data/rules.

| Workload | Observed result |
|---|---|
| Link `";".join(["x"]*2000)` (3,999 chars), three-sample median | m7 **1.88151 s**; current **0.16277 s** |
| HTTP `/api/check`, question EGFR, same answer | **0.44761 s**, HTTP 200 |
| Ordinary EGFR map queued during that check | **0.36605 s**, HTTP 200; ordinary baseline/after about **0.02 s** |
| 400 distinct drug-bearing pieces, `";".join(f"isa x{i:04d}" for i in range(400))` (3,999 chars), cold HTTP check | **2.20771 s**, HTTP 200 |
| Ordinary EGFR queued behind that cold distinct-piece check | **2.12630 s**, HTTP 200 |
| Same distinct-piece check, warm cache | **0.35203 s**, HTTP 200 |

Direct instrumentation confirms **400 cold / 0 warm resolver calls** for the distinct pieces, **1 / 0** for 400 repeated drug-bearing pieces, and **0** for non-drug pieces. Thus repeated-piece caching works; unique legal inputs still consume seconds while `engine.lock` covers resolution/checking (`src/app.py:247-263`). This residual cost is not mislabeled as the repaired m7 defect or a newly introduced slowdown. No timeout/deadlock or global linear-complexity claim is made. [Full reproducible cost evidence](demos_cost/FINDINGS.md).

All seven original HTTP QA demos retain evidence counts **12, 12, 11, 1, 12, 0, 0**. Six complete responses equal original `bfcb83d`; Mvasi differs only in refusal metadata. All 36 page-demo responses equal m7. [Original QA comparison](root_checks/http_demo_results.json), [all demo evidence](demos_cost/FINDINGS.md).

Disease returns **2,774 Trials + 300 Targets**, 30 each, **18,575 bytes in 72.565 ms**. The inherited **24-request / 12-worker** workload returns expected HTTP 200 shapes in **620.588 ms**. These ordinary timings overlapped other audit work and are observations, not latency guarantees. [HTTP results](root_checks/http_results.json).

FILE src/query.py
1: """Grounded question answering over the graph.
2: 
3: The retrieval step is the same resolver the pipeline uses, pointed at the
4: question instead of at a registry record. A question that names "Tagrisso" and a
5: graph that stores CHEMBL3353410 only meet if one vocabulary serves both, so the
6: dictionary is reused rather than reimplemented.
7: 
8: Three properties the answering layer holds to:
9: 
10: * **Every statement is traceable.** Each claim carries the edges it rests on,
11:   each edge carries its source record and fetch time.
12: * **The graph answers, not the model.** With a key, the language model is given
13:   the retrieved evidence and returns claims, not prose; each claim is checked
14:   against the graph and the answer is written from the accepted ones in the
15:   graph's own words (structured.py). With no key the evidence is rendered
16:   directly.
17: * **Nothing unsupported is shipped.** A claim the graph does not support is
18:   reported and left out. An unverifiable answer is worse than a short one.
19: """
20: from __future__ import annotations
21: 
22: import json
23: import os
24: import re
25: import sqlite3
26: import sys
27: import unicodedata
28: from dataclasses import dataclass, field
29: from pathlib import Path
30: 
31: sys.path.insert(0, str(Path(__file__).resolve().parent))
32: import config as C
33: import normalize as N
34: import textrules as T
35: 
36: DB_PATH = C.PROCESSED / "oncokg.sqlite"
37: TRIAL_URL = "https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/{}"
38: 
39: 
40: # --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
41: # evidence
42: # --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
43: @dataclass
44: class Evidence:
45:     statement: str
46:     edge_id: str
47:     relation: str
48:     source: str
49:     source_record: str | None
50:     fetched_at: str | None
51:     confidence: float
52:     tier: str | None = None
53:     url: str | None = None
54:     detail: dict = field(default_factory=dict)
55: 
56:     def citation(self) -> str:
57:         base = self.source_record or self.source
58:         return f"[{base}]"
59: 
60: 
61: @dataclass
62: class Answer:
63:     question: str
64:     intent: str
65:     entities: list[dict]
66:     text: str
67:     evidence: list[Evidence]
68:     generated_by: str
69:     checks: dict
70: 
71:     def to_dict(self) -> dict:
72:         return {
73:             "question": self.question,
74:             "intent": self.intent,
75:             "entities": self.entities,
76:             "answer": self.text,
77:             "generated_by": self.generated_by,
78:             "checks": self.checks,
79:             "evidence": [
80:                 {"statement": e.statement, "relation": e.relation, "edge_id": e.edge_id,
81:                  "source": e.source, "source_record": e.source_record,
82:                  "fetched_at": e.fetched_at, "confidence": e.confidence,
83:                  "resolution_tier": e.tier, "url": e.url, **e.detail}
84:                 for e in self.evidence
85:             ],
86:         }
87: 
88: 
89: # --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
90: # engine
91: # --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
92: # Words that list separately named things; a list item ends at one. "with" is not among
93: # them, because it also sits inside product descriptions ("paclitaxel with albumin bound
94: # nanoparticles").
95: _JOINERS = {"and", "or", "plus", "versus", "vs", "vs.", "+", "&", "then", "followed",
96:             "or/and", "and/or"}
97: 
98: 
99: 
100: # every Unicode dash (category Pd) and minus sign, read as a hyphen
101: _DASHES = "[" + "".join(re.escape(chr(c)) for c in range(0x110000)
102:                         if unicodedata.category(chr(c)) == "Pd" or chr(c) in "\u2212\u207b\u208b") + "]"
103: 
104: 
105: def _fold(text: str) -> str:
106:     return re.sub(_DASHES, "-", unicodedata.normalize("NFKC", text or ""))
107: 
108: def _qualified(words: list[str]) -> bool:
109:     """Does any part of this list item name a distinct product or a tracer?
110: 
111:     The unanchored patterns are tried on the whole item once; the anchored ones (a
112:     biosimilar suffix, a leading isotope) on every run of up to eight words, so the
113:     work grows linearly with the item."""
114:     def hit(text: str) -> bool:
115:         # the alias key ignores punctuation, so the guards also see the text with every
116:         # run of punctuation (not spaces) read as one hyphen ("bevacizumab-(awwb)")
117:         return any(T.is_distinct_product(v) or T.is_radiotracer(v)
118:                    for v in {text, re.sub(r"[^\w\s]+", "-", text).strip("-")})
119: 
120:     if hit(" ".join(words)):
121:         return True
122:     if len(words) > 1:                  # an isotope beside a name; a lone "566C" is a name
123:         if any(T.is_isotope_label(w) for w in words):
124:             return True
125:         if any(T.is_isotope_label(f"{a} {b}") for a, b in zip(words, words[1:])):
126:             return True                 # "89 Zr trastuzumab"
127:     return any(hit(" ".join(words[i:j])) for i in range(len(words))
128:                for j in range(i + 1, min(len(words), i + 8) + 1))
129: 
130: class OncoKG:
131:     def __init__(self, db_path: Path = DB_PATH,
132:                  resolved_path: Path = C.PROCESSED / "resolved.json"):
133:         self.conn = sqlite3.connect(db_path)
134:         self.conn.row_factory = sqlite3.Row
135:         resolved = json.loads(resolved_path.read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
136:         self.dic = resolved["dictionary"]
137:         self.index_keys = list(self.dic["index"].keys())
138:         self._resolve_cache: dict = {}
139:         self.disease = resolved["disease"]
140:         self.targets = {t["symbol"].lower(): t for t in resolved["targets"]
141:                         if t.get("symbol")}
142:         # one name can denote several organisations (a group and a subsidiary
143:         # renamed after it), so a key maps to every matching node, not one.
144:         self.companies: dict[str, list[str]] = {}
145:         for row in self.conn.execute(
146:                 "SELECT id,label FROM node WHERE type='Company' AND retired_at IS NULL"):
147:             self.companies.setdefault(T.canonical_key(row["label"]), []).append(row["id"])
148:             self.companies.setdefault(row["id"].removeprefix("ORG:"), []).append(row["id"])
149:         # a former name ("BeiGene") must still find the merged node
150:         for old, same in N.CURATED_SAME_ENTITY.items():
151:             if same in self.companies:
152:                 self.companies.setdefault(old, []).extend(self.companies[same])
153:         self.companies = {k: sorted(set(v)) for k, v in self.companies.items()}
154: 
155:     # -- entity linking ---------------------------------------------------- #
156:     def link_entities(self, question: str) -> list[dict]:
157:         # one spelling for every check: compatibility forms folded, every dash a hyphen
158:         question = _fold(question)
159:         blocked = self._resolver_blocked(question)
160:         found = self._link(question)
161:         whole = self._whole_name(question, blocked, found)
162:         if whole:
163:             return [whole]
164:         if not blocked:
165:             return found
166:         # a drug the resolver refuses in this text is refused here too, whatever the
167:         # splitting below made of it (the two must agree before anything is linked)
168:         out = [e for e in found if not (e["type"] == "Drug" and e["id"] in blocked)]
169:         for text in sorted({t for t in blocked.values()}):
170:             if not any(e["type"] == "Refused" and (text in e["label"] or e["label"] in text)
171:                        for e in out):
172:                 out.append({"type": "Refused", "id": None, "label": text, "matched_text": text,
173:                             "why": "names a distinct product or tracer, not the "
174:                                    "originator molecule"})
175:         return out
176: 
177:     def _resolver_blocked(self, text: str) -> dict:
178:         """Drug ids inside the pieces the intervention resolver refuses as a distinct
179:         product or a tracer, with the refused piece."""
180:         comps = []
181:         lone = set()
182:         pieces = {p.strip() for p in re.split(r"[,;\uff0c\uff1b\u3001/|]", text) if p.strip()}
183:         for piece in pieces:                         # each distinct list piece once
184:             words = [w for w in re.split(r"[^\w\-]+", piece) if w]
185:             if not self._drug_spans(words, set(), [0] * len(words)):
186:                 continue                             # no drug in it: nothing to refuse
187:             got = self._resolve_cache.get(piece)
188:             if got is None:
189:                 try:
190:                     got = N.resolve_drug_string(piece, self.dic, self.index_keys)["components"]
191:                 except Exception:                    # never let a guard crash a lookup
192:                     got = []
193:                 if len(self._resolve_cache) >= 4096:
194:                     self._resolve_cache.clear()
195:                 self._resolve_cache[piece] = got
196:             if len(words) == 1 and T.canonical_key(piece) in self.dic["index"]:
197:                 lone.add(piece)                      # one stored name on its own ("566C")
198:             comps += got
199:         out: dict = {}
200:         accepted = {c.get("drug_id") for c in comps
201:                     if c.get("drug_id") and not str(c.get("tier", "")).startswith("BLOCKED")}
202:         for c in comps:
203:             piece = c.get("component") or ""
204:             tracer = "radiotracer_guard" in (c.get("rules") or []) or T.is_radiotracer(piece)
205:             if not (c.get("tier") == "BLOCKED_DISTINCT_PRODUCT"
206:                     or (str(c.get("tier", "")).startswith("BLOCKED") and tracer)):
207:                 continue
208:             if piece in lone:
209:                 continue
210:             words = [w for w in re.split(r"[\s/|,;]+", piece) if w]
211:             words = [w.strip(" ?.!:()[]") or w for w in words]
212:             for a, b in self._drug_spans(words, set(), [0] * len(words)):
213:                 key = T.canonical_key(" ".join(words[a:b]))
214:                 for h in self.dic["index"].get(key, []):
215:                     # the same molecule named separately and accepted ("biosimilar
216:                     # anastrozole, Arimidex") is left to the list-item rule below
217:                     if h["drug_id"] not in accepted:
218:                         out[h["drug_id"]] = piece
219:         return out
220: 
221:     def _whole_name(self, text: str, blocked: dict, found: list):
222:         """The whole input is exactly one stored drug name, letter for letter ("CP-358,774",
223:         "(E/Z)-AFATINIB"): read it whole rather than as the list its punctuation suggests.
224: 
225:         Only after every refusal has had its say: nothing the resolver or the list
226:         linker refused, and no product or tracer pattern in the name."""
227:         if blocked or any(e["type"] == "Refused" for e in found):
228:             return None
229:         name = text.strip(" ?!.")
230:         if T.is_distinct_product(name) or T.is_radiotracer(name):
231:             return None             # the list linker above has already applied the rest
232:         key = T.canonical_key(name)
233:         exact = [h for h in (self.dic["index"].get(key) or [])
234:                  if _fold(h["alias"]).strip(" ?!.").lower() == name.lower()]
235:         if not exact or len({h["drug_id"] for h in exact}) != 1:
236:             return None
237:         did = exact[0]["drug_id"]
238:         return {"type": "Drug", "id": did, "label": self.dic["drugs"][did]["name"],
239:                 "matched_text": name, "matched_alias": exact[0]["alias"]}
240: 
241:     def _link(self, question: str) -> list[dict]:
242:         found: list[dict] = []
243:         seen: set = set()
244:         tokens: list[str] = []
245:         clause: list[int] = []          # which list piece each token is in
246:         inner: list[bool] = []          # token inside parentheses
247:         ci, depth = 0, 0
248:         for piece in re.split(r"[,;\uff0c\uff1b\u3001]", question):
249:             for raw in re.split(r"\s+", piece):
250:                 # a slash or bar, glued or free, ends the piece ("bevacizumab - awwb/
251:                 # osimertinib"); it must not hide a product suffix from the guards
252:                 for j, part in enumerate(re.split(r"[/|]", raw)):
253:                     if j:
254:                         ci += 1
255:                     if not part:
256:                         continue
257:                     if re.search(r"\w", part):
258:                         # edge punctuation and wrappers off ("bevacizumab-awwb?",
259:                         # "(bevacizumab-awwb)"); an isotope ("[89Zr]") is checked as a word
260:                         t = re.sub(r"^[^\w]+|[^\w]+$", "", part)
261:                     elif part in ("-", "\u2013", "\u2014", "+", "&"):
262:                         t = part            # "bevacizumab - awwb" needs its dash
263:                     else:
264:                         t = ""
265:                     if t:
266:                         tokens.append(t)
267:                         clause.append(ci)
268:                         inner.append(depth > 0 or part.startswith("("))
269:                     depth = max(0, depth + part.count("(") - part.count(")"))
270:             ci += 1
271: 
272:         # The resolver's refusals apply to questions too: "Mvasi", "liposomal
273:         # doxorubicin" or "generic version of bevacizumab" must never answer as the
274:         # originator molecule. The text is cut into list items at commas, semicolons
275:         # and listing words ("and", "or", "plus", ...). An item that names a distinct
276:         # product or a tracer anywhere has every drug in it refused: which drug a
277:         # qualifier describes is not guessed, so "osimertinib with generic version of
278:         # bevacizumab" refuses both, while "osimertinib and generic version of
279:         # bevacizumab" keeps osimertinib. Targets and companies in the item still link.
280:         seg: list[int] = []
281:         n = 0
282:         for k, t in enumerate(tokens):
283:             # "and" inside parentheses describes one product ("doxorubicin
284:             # (nanoparticle and liposomal formulation)"); it does not list two
285:             if t.lower() in _JOINERS and not inner[k]:
286:                 n += 1
287:                 seg.append(-1)
288:                 continue
289:             if k and clause[k] != clause[k - 1]:
290:                 n += 1
291:             seg.append(n)
292:         refused: set[int] = {k for k, t in enumerate(tokens)
293:                              if T.is_distinct_product(t) or T.is_radiotracer(t)}
294:         drugs = self._drug_spans(tokens, {k for k in range(len(tokens)) if seg[k] < 0}, seg)
295:         dangling = None                 # a qualifier item with no drug, joined by "and"/"or"
296:         prev_end = -1
297:         for sid in sorted(set(seg) - {-1}):
298:             idx = [k for k in range(len(tokens)) if seg[k] == sid]
299:             text = " ".join(tokens[k] for k in idx)
300:             joined = (dangling is not None and idx[0] > prev_end + 1
301:                       and all(seg[k] < 0 for k in range(prev_end + 1, idx[0]))
302:                       and clause[idx[0]] == clause[prev_end])
303:             prev_end = idx[-1]
304:             hit = [k for a, b in drugs if seg[a] == sid for k in range(a, b)]
305:             if _qualified([tokens[k] for k in idx]):
306:                 named = any(k in refused and (
307:                     T.canonical_key(tokens[k]) in self.dic["index"]
308:                     or tokens[k].lower() in T.BIOSIMILAR_BRANDS | T.REFORMULATION_NAMES)
309:                     for k in idx)
310:                 if not hit and not named:
311:                     # "liposomal and nanoparticle formulation of doxorubicin": a bare
312:                     # qualifier carries over a listing word to the next item's drug; a
313:                     # product named by itself ("Mvasi and bevacizumab") does not
314:                     dangling = text
315:                     continue
316:                 dangling = None
317:             elif joined and hit:
318:                 text = f"{dangling} ... {text}"
319:                 dangling = None
320:             else:
321:                 dangling = None if not joined else dangling
322:                 continue
323:             refused.update(hit)
324:             found.append({"type": "Refused", "id": None, "label": text,
325:                           "matched_text": text,
326:                           "why": "names a distinct product or tracer, not "
327:                                  "the originator molecule"})
328: 
329:         matched: set[int] = set()
330:         for size in range(min(5, len(tokens)), 0, -1):
331:             for i in range(len(tokens) - size + 1):
332:                 if (refused | matched) & set(range(i, i + size)):
333:                     continue
334:                 if size > 1 and (len(set(seg[i:i + size])) > 1 or min(seg[i:i + size]) < 0):
335:                     continue            # a name never spans two list items
336:                 window = " ".join(tokens[i:i + size])
337:                 if len(window) < 3:
338:                     continue
339:                 key = T.canonical_key(window)
340: 
341:                 hits = self.dic["index"].get(key)
342:                 # Several words only make a drug name when the name itself has
343:                 # several words. The key drops spaces, so "is a" read as the
344:                 # abbreviation "isa" (isatuximab) in any question or answer.
345:                 if hits and size > 1 and not re.search(r"[\s\-/]", hits[0]["alias"]):
346:                     hits = None
347:                 if hits and len({h["drug_id"] for h in hits}) == 1:
348:                     did = hits[0]["drug_id"]
349:                     if ("Drug", did) not in seen:
350:                         seen.add(("Drug", did))
351:                         found.append({"type": "Drug", "id": did,
352:                                       "label": self.dic["drugs"][did]["name"],
353:                                       "matched_text": window,
354:                                       "matched_alias": hits[0]["alias"]})
355:                     matched.update(range(i, i + size))   # words of a longer name are not read again

FILE src/app.py
1: """A local page for trying the pipeline by hand.
2: 
3:     python src/app.py            then open http://localhost:8765
4: 
5: A relation map (a node and the nodes live edges tie it to, a capped list per relation,
6: click to move on),
7: then four boxes: type an intervention string the way a registry would write it and see
8: how the resolver reads it, or ask a question and see the grounded answer with its
9: evidence, give a structured answer (claims citing evidence edges) and see which
10: claims the graph supports and the answer written from them, or paste free prose and
11: see what the auxiliary word-order check flags. All of them call
12: the functions the pipeline uses (resolve_drug_string, OncoKG.answer,
13: OncoKG.check_groundedness) on the built artefacts, so what the page shows is what the
14: pipeline would do. Nothing is written; the language model is never called.
15: """
16: from __future__ import annotations
17: 
18: import json
19: import sqlite3
20: import sys
21: import threading
22: from http.server import BaseHTTPRequestHandler, ThreadingHTTPServer
23: from pathlib import Path
24: 
25: sys.path.insert(0, str(Path(__file__).resolve().parent))
26: import config as C
27: import normalize as N
28: import textrules as T
29: from query import OncoKG
30: 
31: PAGE = C.WEB / "try.html"
32: PORT = 8765
33: 
34: 
35: class Engine:
36:     def __init__(self):
37:         self.kg = OncoKG()
38:         # A browser opens spare connections it may never use, which would stall a
39:         # single-threaded server; requests are threaded, the engine is not.
40:         self.kg.conn.close()
41:         self.kg.conn = sqlite3.connect(C.PROCESSED / "oncokg.sqlite", check_same_thread=False)
42:         self.kg.conn.row_factory = sqlite3.Row
43:         self.lock = threading.Lock()
44:         self.dic = self.kg.dic
45:         self.index_keys = self.kg.index_keys
46:         target_rows, _, _ = N._load("targets")
47:         self.target_keys = N.target_guard_keys(target_rows, self.dic)
48: 
49:     def resolve(self, text: str) -> dict:
50:         out = N.resolve_drug_string(text, self.dic, self.index_keys, self.target_keys)
51:         for c in out["components"]:
52:             d = self.dic["drugs"].get(c.get("drug_id") or "")
53:             if d:
54:                 c["drug"] = {k: d.get(k) for k in
55:                              ("name", "drug_type", "max_phase_overall", "max_phase_in_nsclc")}
56:                 c["drug"]["mechanisms"] = [m.get("mechanismOfAction") if isinstance(m, dict) else m
57:                                            for m in (d.get("mechanisms") or [])][:3]
58:                 c["drug"]["trials"] = self.kg.conn.execute(
59:                     "SELECT COUNT(*) FROM edge WHERE rel='EVALUATES' AND dst=? AND retired_at IS NULL",
60:                     (d["drug_id"],)).fetchone()[0]
61:                 par = self.dic["drugs"].get(d.get("parent_molecule") or "")
62:                 if par:
63:                     c["drug"]["parent"] = {"name": par["name"], "trials": self.kg.conn.execute(
64:                         "SELECT COUNT(*) FROM edge WHERE rel='EVALUATES' AND dst=? AND retired_at IS NULL",
65:                         (par["drug_id"],)).fetchone()[0]}
66:             if c.get("candidates"):
67:                 c["candidate_names"] = [
68:                     (self.dic["drugs"].get(x) or {}).get("name", x) for x in c["candidates"]]
69:         return out
70: 
71:     def explore(self, text: str, per_group: int = 30) -> dict:
72:         """One live node and the live nodes its live edges tie it to, grouped by relation.
73: 
74:         `text` is a node id or a name the question linker reads as one entity. A name
75:         the linker refuses (a biosimilar, a reformulation, a tracer) is reported, never
76:         mapped to the originator. Each group keeps `per_group` neighbours, sorted as the
77:         page states: trials by start date (latest first), drugs by trials in the graph,
78:         sponsors by trials they lead, disease targets by association score. Sponsorship
79:         keeps its role (lead sponsor or collaborator)."""
80:         conn = self.kg.conn
81:         also: list = []
82:         matched = None
83:         row = conn.execute("SELECT id FROM node WHERE id=? AND retired_at IS NULL",
84:                            (text,)).fetchone()
85:         if row:
86:             nid = row["id"]
87:         else:
88:             ents = self.kg.link_entities(text)
89:             refused = [e for e in ents if e["type"] == "Refused"]
90:             hits = [e for e in ents if e.get("id") and e["type"] != "Refused"]
91:             if not hits:
92:                 if refused:
93:                     return {"refused": refused[0]["label"], "why": refused[0]["why"]}
94:                 return {"not_found": text}
95:             nid, matched = hits[0]["id"], hits[0].get("matched_alias") or hits[0].get("matched_text")
96:             also = [{"id": e["id"], "type": e["type"], "label": e["label"]} for e in hits[1:]]
97:             for oid in hits[0].get("also_matching_ids") or []:   # one alias, several ids
98:                 lab = conn.execute("SELECT type, label FROM node WHERE id=? AND retired_at IS NULL",
99:                                    (oid,)).fetchone()
100:                 if lab and oid != nid:
101:                     also.append({"id": oid, "type": lab["type"], "label": lab["label"]})
102:             # the same words may also name an organisation or a target the linker
103:             # passed over because a drug alias came first ("Roche" is a folic acid alias)
104:             key = T.canonical_key(text)
105:             for cid in self.kg.companies.get(key, []):
106:                 if cid != nid and all(x.get("id") != cid for x in also):
107:                     lab = conn.execute("SELECT label FROM node WHERE id=? AND retired_at IS NULL",
108:                                        (cid,)).fetchone()
109:                     if lab:
110:                         also.append({"id": cid, "type": "Company", "label": lab["label"]})
111:             t = self.kg.targets.get(text.strip().lower())
112:             if t and t["target_id"] != nid:
113:                 also.append({"id": t["target_id"], "type": "Target", "label": t["symbol"]})
114:             also += [{"refused": e["label"]} for e in refused]
115:         node = conn.execute("SELECT id, type, label, props FROM node WHERE id=? "
116:                             "AND retired_at IS NULL", (nid,)).fetchone()
117:         if node is None:                              # a name can outlive its node
118:             return {"not_found": text}
119:         rows = conn.execute(
120:             "SELECT e.id AS eid, e.rel, e.src, e.props AS eprops, n.id, n.type, n.label, "
121:             "n.props AS nprops FROM edge e JOIN node n "
122:             "ON n.id = CASE WHEN e.src=? THEN e.dst ELSE e.src END "
123:             "WHERE (e.src=? OR e.dst=?) AND e.retired_at IS NULL AND n.retired_at IS NULL",
124:             (nid, nid, nid)).fetchall()
125:         drug_ids = list({r["id"] for r in rows if r["type"] == "Drug"} | {nid})
126:         trials = {}
127:         for i in range(0, len(drug_ids), 500):
128:             chunk = drug_ids[i:i + 500]
129:             trials.update(conn.execute(               # live trials only
130:                 "SELECT e.dst, COUNT(*) FROM edge e JOIN node t ON t.id = e.src "
131:                 "WHERE e.rel='EVALUATES' AND e.retired_at IS NULL AND t.retired_at IS NULL "
132:                 f"AND e.dst IN ({','.join('?' * len(chunk))}) GROUP BY e.dst", chunk).fetchall())
133:         groups: dict = {}
134:         for r in rows:
135:             ep, np_ = _obj(r["eprops"]), _obj(r["nprops"])
136:             item = {"id": r["id"], "type": r["type"], "label": r["label"], "edge_id": r["eid"]}
137:             role = ep.get("role") if r["rel"] == "SPONSORS" else None
138:             if r["type"] == "Drug":
139:                 item["trials"] = trials.get(r["id"], 0)
140:                 item["max_phase"] = np_.get("max_phase_overall")
141:             if r["rel"] == "SPONSORS_TRIAL_OF":
142:                 n = ep.get("evidence_trials")
143:                 # a count the edge does not record stays unknown; 0 is only for sorting
144:                 item["evidence_trials"] = n if _number(n) else None
145:                 item["weight"] = n if _number(n) else 0
146:                 item["sponsor_class"] = ep.get("sponsor_class")
147:             elif r["type"] == "Trial":
148:                 item.update({k: np_.get(k) for k in ("phases", "status", "title", "start_date", "url")})
149:                 item["weight"] = str(np_.get("start_date") or "")
150:             elif r["type"] == "Drug":
151:                 item["weight"] = item["trials"]
152:             elif r["rel"] == "ASSOCIATED_WITH":
153:                 score = ep.get("association_score")
154:                 item["score"] = score if _number(score) else None
155:                 item["weight"] = item["score"] or 0
156:             else:
157:                 item["weight"] = 0
158:             if r["rel"] == "ACTS_ON":
159:                 item["action"] = ep.get("action_type")
160:             if role:
161:                 item["role"] = role
162:             key = (r["rel"], "out" if r["src"] == nid else "in", r["type"], role)
163:             groups.setdefault(key, []).append(item)
164:         out = []
165:         for (rel, direction, ntype, role), items in groups.items():
166:             items.sort(key=lambda x: x["label"] or "")              # names A to Z
167:             items.sort(key=lambda x: x["weight"], reverse=True)     # then by the stated weight
168:             out.append({"rel": rel, "dir": direction, "type": ntype, "role": role,
169:                         "total": len(items), "items": items[:per_group]})
170:         out.sort(key=lambda g: -g["total"])
171:         props = _obj(node["props"])
172:         return {"center": {"id": node["id"], "type": node["type"], "label": node["label"],
173:                            "matched_text": matched,
174:                            "trials": trials.get(node["id"], 0) if node["type"] == "Drug" else None,
175:                            "props": {k: props.get(k) for k in
176:                                      ("max_phase_overall", "drug_type", "phases", "status",
177:                                       "title", "url", "start_date") if props.get(k) is not None}},
178:                 "also": also, "groups": out}
179: 
180:     def ask(self, question: str) -> dict:
181:         return self.kg.answer(question, use_llm=False).to_dict()
182: 
183:     def structured(self, question: str, payload: str) -> dict:
184:         """Check a structured answer (claims citing edges) and write the answer from it."""
185:         kg = self.kg
186:         entities = kg.link_entities(question)
187:         evidence = kg.retrieve(kg.classify(question, entities), entities)
188:         res = kg.verify_structured(payload, evidence)
189:         res["evidence"] = [{"edge_id": e.edge_id, "statement": e.statement} for e in evidence]
190:         return res
191: 
192:     def check(self, question: str, answer: str) -> dict:
193:         """Check an answer someone else wrote against the evidence for the question."""
194:         kg = self.kg
195:         entities = kg.link_entities(question)
196:         intent = kg.classify(question, entities)
197:         evidence = kg.retrieve(intent, entities)
198:         checks = kg.check_groundedness(answer, evidence, entities, generated=True,
199:                                        vocabulary=set(self.dic["index"]),
200:                                        linker=kg.link_entities)
201:         return {"intent": intent, "entities": entities, "checks": checks,
202:                 "evidence": [{"statement": e.statement, "source_record": e.source_record,
203:                               "source": e.source, "url": e.url} for e in evidence[:8]],
204:                 "evidence_count": len(evidence)}
205: 
206: 
207: def _number(v) -> bool:
208:     return isinstance(v, (int, float)) and not isinstance(v, bool) and v == v
209: 
210: 
211: def _obj(raw) -> dict:
212:     """Stored props as a dict; anything else (null, a list, broken JSON) as empty."""
213:     try:
214:         v = json.loads(raw) if raw else {}
215:     except ValueError:
216:         return {}
217:     return v if isinstance(v, dict) else {}
218: 
219: 
220: def make_handler(engine: Engine):
221:     class Handler(BaseHTTPRequestHandler):
222:         def _send(self, code: int, body: bytes, ctype: str):
223:             self.send_response(code)
224:             self.send_header("Content-Type", ctype)
225:             self.send_header("Content-Length", str(len(body)))
226:             self.end_headers()
227:             self.wfile.write(body)
228: 
229:         def do_GET(self):
230:             if self.path in ("/", "/index.html"):
231:                 self._send(200, PAGE.read_bytes(), "text/html; charset=utf-8")
232:             else:
233:                 self._send(404, b"not found", "text/plain")
234: 
235:         def do_POST(self):
236:             try:
237:                 n = int(self.headers.get("Content-Length") or 0)
238:                 body = json.loads(self.rfile.read(min(n, 20000)) or b"{}")
239:                 text = str(body.get("text") or "").strip()
240:                 if len(text) > 500:                      # refused, not silently cut
241:                     raise ValueError("input is longer than 500 characters")
242:                 if not text:
243:                     raise ValueError("empty input")
244:                 if self.path not in ("/api/resolve", "/api/ask", "/api/check",
245:                                      "/api/structured", "/api/explore"):
246:                     return self._send(404, b"{}", "application/json")
247:                 with engine.lock:
248:                     if self.path == "/api/explore":
249:                         res = engine.explore(text)
250:                     elif self.path == "/api/resolve":
251:                         res = engine.resolve(text)
252:                     elif self.path == "/api/ask":
253:                         res = engine.ask(text)
254:                     elif self.path == "/api/structured":
255:                         answer = str(body.get("answer") or "").strip()[:20000]
256:                         if not answer:
257:                             raise ValueError("empty answer")
258:                         res = engine.structured(text, answer)
259:                     else:
260:                         answer = str(body.get("answer") or "").strip()[:4000]
261:                         if not answer:
262:                             raise ValueError("empty answer")
263:                         res = engine.check(text, answer)
264:                 self._send(200, json.dumps(res, ensure_ascii=False,
265:                                            allow_nan=False).encode("utf-8"),
266:                            "application/json; charset=utf-8")
267:             except Exception as e:  # show the reason on the page, not a dead request
268:                 self._send(400, json.dumps({"error": str(e)}, ensure_ascii=False).encode("utf-8"),
269:                            "application/json; charset=utf-8")
270: 
271:         def log_message(self, *a):
272:             pass
273:     return Handler
274: 
275: 
276: def main() -> None:
277:     port = int(sys.argv[1]) if len(sys.argv) > 1 else PORT
278:     engine = Engine()
279:     print(f"OncoKG try-it page: http://localhost:{port}", flush=True)
280:     ThreadingHTTPServer(("127.0.0.1", port), make_handler(engine)).serve_forever()
281: 
282: 
283: if __name__ == "__main__":
284:     main()

FILE audit_evidence_claims/m8/root_checks/run_replays.py
1: """Execute inherited reproducers, owning and closing one HTTP port at a time."""
2: import json,os,subprocess,sys,time
3: from pathlib import Path
4: OUT=Path(__file__).resolve().parent
5: ROOT=OUT.parents[2]
6: env={**os.environ,'PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE':'1','PYTHONUTF8':'1','PYTHONIOENCODING':'utf-8',
7:      'TMP':str(OUT/'tmp'),'TEMP':str(OUT/'tmp'),'TMPDIR':str(OUT/'tmp')}
8: names=(['run_verification.py'] if 'tests' in sys.argv else ['perf_probe.py'] if 'perf' in sys.argv
9:        else ['run_http.py','supplemental_http.py','attack_http.py','m5_http.py','cross_item_http.py','perf_check_http.py'])
10: rows=[]
11: for name in names:
12:     start=time.perf_counter()
13:     p=subprocess.run([sys.executable,'-B',str(OUT/name)],cwd=ROOT,env=env,capture_output=True,timeout=240)
14:     (OUT/(name+'.stdout.txt')).write_bytes(p.stdout+p.stderr)
15:     rows.append({'script':name,'exit_code':p.returncode,'seconds':time.perf_counter()-start})
16:     (OUT/('execution_'+sys.argv[-1]+'.json')).write_text(json.dumps(rows,indent=2),encoding='utf-8')
17:     print(json.dumps(rows[-1]),flush=True)
18:     if p.returncode: raise SystemExit(p.returncode)

FILE audit_evidence_claims/m8/root_checks/run_verification.py
1: """Run the unmodified existing suite with scratch confined to this directory."""
2: import hashlib
3: import json
4: import os
5: from pathlib import Path
6: import subprocess
7: import sys
8: import time
9: 
10: OUT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent
11: ROOT = OUT.parents[2]
12: TMP = OUT / "tmp"
13: TMP.mkdir(exist_ok=True)
14: env = {**os.environ, "PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE": "1", "PYTHONUTF8": "1",
15:        "PYTHONIOENCODING": "utf-8", "TMP": str(TMP), "TEMP": str(TMP),
16:        "TMPDIR": str(TMP)}
17: 
18: def git(*args):
19:     result = subprocess.run(["git", *args], cwd=ROOT, capture_output=True)
20:     return {"code": result.returncode, "stdout": result.stdout.decode("utf-8", "replace"),
21:             "stderr": result.stderr.decode("utf-8", "replace")}
22: 
23: files = ["src/app.py", "src/query.py", "web/try.html", "tests/test_app.py",
24:          "data/processed/oncokg.sqlite"]
25: before = {p: hashlib.sha256((ROOT / p).read_bytes()).hexdigest() for p in files}
26: (OUT / "verification_initial.json").write_text(json.dumps({"head": git("rev-parse", "HEAD"),
27:     "status": git("status", "--short"), "hashes": before}, indent=2), encoding="utf-8")
28: cmd = [sys.executable, "-m", "pytest", "tests", "-q", "-p", "no:cacheprovider"]
29: start = time.perf_counter()
30: run = subprocess.run(cmd, cwd=ROOT, env=env, capture_output=True)
31: (OUT / "pytest.txt").write_bytes(run.stdout + run.stderr)
32: after = {p: hashlib.sha256((ROOT / p).read_bytes()).hexdigest() for p in files}
33: summary = {"command": "python -m pytest tests -q -p no:cacheprovider",
34:            "environment": {k: env[k] for k in ("PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE", "PYTHONUTF8", "PYTHONIOENCODING", "TMP", "TEMP", "TMPDIR")},
35:            "exit_code": run.returncode, "wall_seconds": round(time.perf_counter() - start, 3),
36:            "hashes_unchanged": before == after, "hashes_after": after}
37: (OUT / "pytest_summary.json").write_text(json.dumps(summary, indent=2), encoding="utf-8")
38: print((run.stdout + run.stderr).decode("utf-8", "replace"))
39: print(json.dumps(summary, indent=2))
40: raise SystemExit(run.returncode)

FILE audit_evidence_claims/m8/root_checks/run_http.py
1: """Round 5 HTTP and QA regression checks; all output stays beside this file."""
2: import ast
3: import hashlib
4: import json
5: import os
6: from pathlib import Path
7: import re
8: import socket
9: import subprocess
10: import sys
11: import time
12: import types
13: import urllib.error
14: import urllib.request
15: 
16: OUT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent
17: ROOT = OUT.parents[2]
18: PORT = 8931
19: BASE = f"http://127.0.0.1:{PORT}"
20: sys.path.insert(0, str(ROOT / "src"))
21: os.environ["PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE"] = "1"
22: DB = ROOT / "data/processed/oncokg.sqlite"
23: 
24: def git(*args):
25:     p = subprocess.run(["git", *args], cwd=ROOT, capture_output=True)
26:     return {"code": p.returncode, "stdout": p.stdout.decode("utf-8", "replace"),
27:             "stderr": p.stderr.decode("utf-8", "replace")}
28: 
29: def post(path, payload):
30:     req = urllib.request.Request(BASE + path, data=json.dumps(payload).encode(),
31:                                  headers={"Content-Type": "application/json"})
32:     try:
33:         res = urllib.request.urlopen(req, timeout=30)
34:     except urllib.error.HTTPError as e:
35:         res = e
36:     return {"status": res.status, "body": json.loads(res.read())}
37: 
38: before = hashlib.sha256(DB.read_bytes()).hexdigest()
39: initial = {"head": git("rev-parse", "HEAD"), "status": git("status", "--short"),
40:            "db_sha256": before}
41: (OUT / "initial_integrity.json").write_text(json.dumps(initial, indent=2), encoding="utf-8")
42: with socket.socket() as s:
43:     assert s.connect_ex(("127.0.0.1", PORT)) != 0, "audit port already occupied"
44: 
45: log = (OUT / "server.txt").open("w", encoding="utf-8")
46: proc = subprocess.Popen([sys.executable, "-B", "-u", "src/app.py", str(PORT)], cwd=ROOT,
47:                         env=os.environ.copy(), stdout=log, stderr=subprocess.STDOUT,
48:                         creationflags=getattr(subprocess, "CREATE_NO_WINDOW", 0))
49: try:
50:     for _ in range(100):
51:         if proc.poll() is not None:
52:             raise RuntimeError("server exited")
53:         try:

221:     def _whole_name(self, text: str, blocked: dict, found: list):
222:         """The whole input is exactly one stored drug name, letter for letter ("CP-358,774",
223:         "(E/Z)-AFATINIB"): read it whole rather than as the list its punctuation suggests.
224: 
225:         Only after every refusal has had its say: nothing the resolver or the list
226:         linker refused, and no product or tracer pattern in the name."""
227:         if blocked or any(e["type"] == "Refused" for e in found):
228:             return None
229:         name = text.strip(" ?!.")
230:         if T.is_distinct_product(name) or T.is_radiotracer(name):
231:             return None             # the list linker above has already applied the rest
232:         key = T.canonical_key(name)
233:         exact = [h for h in (self.dic["index"].get(key) or [])
234:                  if _fold(h["alias"]).strip(" ?!.").lower() == name.lower()]
235:         if not exact or len({h["drug_id"] for h in exact}) != 1:
236:             return None
237:         did = exact[0]["drug_id"]
238:         return {"type": "Drug", "id": did, "label": self.dic["drugs"][did]["name"],
239:                 "matched_text": name, "matched_alias": exact[0]["alias"]}
240: 
241:     def _link(self, question: str) -> list[dict]:
242:         found: list[dict] = []
243:         seen: set = set()
244:         tokens: list[str] = []
245:         clause: list[int] = []          # which list piece each token is in
246:         inner: list[bool] = []          # token inside parentheses
247:         ci, depth = 0, 0
248:         for piece in re.split(r"[,;\uff0c\uff1b\u3001]", question):
249:             for raw in re.split(r"\s+", piece):
250:                 # a slash or bar, glued or free, ends the piece ("bevacizumab - awwb/
251:                 # osimertinib"); it must not hide a product suffix from the guards
252:                 for j, part in enumerate(re.split(r"[/|]", raw)):
253:                     if j:
254:                         ci += 1
255:                     if not part:
256:                         continue
257:                     if re.search(r"\w", part):
258:                         # edge punctuation and wrappers off ("bevacizumab-awwb?",
259:                         # "(bevacizumab-awwb)"); an isotope ("[89Zr]") is checked as a word
260:                         t = re.sub(r"^[^\w]+|[^\w]+$", "", part)
261:                     elif part in ("-", "\u2013", "\u2014", "+", "&"):
262:                         t = part            # "bevacizumab - awwb" needs its dash
263:                     else:
264:                         t = ""
265:                     if t:
266:                         tokens.append(t)
267:                         clause.append(ci)
268:                         inner.append(depth > 0 or part.startswith("("))
269:                     depth = max(0, depth + part.count("(") - part.count(")"))
270:             ci += 1
271: 
272:         # The resolver's refusals apply to questions too: "Mvasi", "liposomal
273:         # doxorubicin" or "generic version of bevacizumab" must never answer as the
274:         # originator molecule. The text is cut into list items at commas, semicolons
275:         # and listing words ("and", "or", "plus", ...). An item that names a distinct
276:         # product or a tracer anywhere has every drug in it refused: which drug a
277:         # qualifier describes is not guessed, so "osimertinib with generic version of
278:         # bevacizumab" refuses both, while "osimertinib and generic version of
279:         # bevacizumab" keeps osimertinib. Targets and companies in the item still link.
280:         seg: list[int] = []
281:         n = 0
282:         for k, t in enumerate(tokens):
283:             # "and" inside parentheses describes one product ("doxorubicin
284:             # (nanoparticle and liposomal formulation)"); it does not list two
285:             if t.lower() in _JOINERS and not inner[k]:
286:                 n += 1
287:                 seg.append(-1)
288:                 continue
289:             if k and clause[k] != clause[k - 1]:
290:                 n += 1
291:             seg.append(n)
292:         refused: set[int] = {k for k, t in enumerate(tokens)
293:                              if T.is_distinct_product(t) or T.is_radiotracer(t)}
294:         drugs = self._drug_spans(tokens, {k for k in range(len(tokens)) if seg[k] < 0}, seg)
295:         dangling = None                 # a qualifier item with no drug, joined by "and"/"or"
296:         prev_end = -1
297:         for sid in sorted(set(seg) - {-1}):
298:             idx = [k for k in range(len(tokens)) if seg[k] == sid]
299:             text = " ".join(tokens[k] for k in idx)
300:             joined = (dangling is not None and idx[0] > prev_end + 1
301:                       and all(seg[k] < 0 for k in range(prev_end + 1, idx[0]))
302:                       and clause[idx[0]] == clause[prev_end])
303:             prev_end = idx[-1]
304:             hit = [k for a, b in drugs if seg[a] == sid for k in range(a, b)]
305:             if _qualified([tokens[k] for k in idx]):
306:                 named = any(k in refused and (
307:                     T.canonical_key(tokens[k]) in self.dic["index"]
308:                     or tokens[k].lower() in T.BIOSIMILAR_BRANDS | T.REFORMULATION_NAMES)
309:                     for k in idx)
310:                 if not hit and not named:
311:                     # "liposomal and nanoparticle formulation of doxorubicin": a bare
312:                     # qualifier carries over a listing word to the next item's drug; a
313:                     # product named by itself ("Mvasi and bevacizumab") does not
314:                     dangling = text
315:                     continue
316:                 dangling = None
317:             elif joined and hit:
318:                 text = f"{dangling} ... {text}"
319:                 dangling = None
320:             else:
321:                 dangling = None if not joined else dangling
322:                 continue
323:             refused.update(hit)
324:             found.append({"type": "Refused", "id": None, "label": text,
325:                           "matched_text": text,
326:                           "why": "names a distinct product or tracer, not "
327:                                  "the originator molecule"})
328: 
329:         matched: set[int] = set()
330:         for size in range(min(5, len(tokens)), 0, -1):
331:             for i in range(len(tokens) - size + 1):
332:                 if (refused | matched) & set(range(i, i + size)):
333:                     continue
334:                 if size > 1 and (len(set(seg[i:i + size])) > 1 or min(seg[i:i + size]) < 0):
335:                     continue            # a name never spans two list items
336:                 window = " ".join(tokens[i:i + size])
337:                 if len(window) < 3:
338:                     continue
339:                 key = T.canonical_key(window)
340: 
341:                 hits = self.dic["index"].get(key)
342:                 # Several words only make a drug name when the name itself has
343:                 # several words. The key drops spaces, so "is a" read as the
344:                 # abbreviation "isa" (isatuximab) in any question or answer.
345:                 if hits and size > 1 and not re.search(r"[\s\-/]", hits[0]["alias"]):
346:                     hits = None
347:                 if hits and len({h["drug_id"] for h in hits}) == 1:
348:                     did = hits[0]["drug_id"]
349:                     if ("Drug", did) not in seen:
350:                         seen.add(("Drug", did))
351:                         found.append({"type": "Drug", "id": did,
352:                                       "label": self.dic["drugs"][did]["name"],
353:                                       "matched_text": window,
354:                                       "matched_alias": hits[0]["alias"]})
355:                     matched.update(range(i, i + size))   # words of a longer name are not read again
356:                     continue
357: 
358:                 if window.lower() in self.targets:
359:                     t = self.targets[window.lower()]
360:                     if ("Target", t["target_id"]) not in seen:
361:                         seen.add(("Target", t["target_id"]))
362:                         found.append({"type": "Target", "id": t["target_id"],
363:                                       "label": t["symbol"], "matched_text": window})
364:                     matched.update(range(i, i + size))   # words of a longer name are not read again
365:                     continue
366: 
367:                 if key in self.companies:
368:                     cid = self.companies[key][0]
369:                     if ("Company", cid) not in seen:
370:                         seen.add(("Company", cid))
371:                         row = self.conn.execute(
372:                             "SELECT label FROM node WHERE id=?", (cid,)).fetchone()
373:                         found.append({"type": "Company", "id": cid,
374:                                       "label": row["label"], "matched_text": window,
375:                                       "also_matching_ids": self.companies[key][1:]})
376:                     matched.update(range(i, i + size))   # words of a longer name are not read again
377:                     continue
378: 
379:                 if key in self.disease["index"]:
380:                     if ("Disease", self.disease["disease_id"]) not in seen:
381:                         seen.add(("Disease", self.disease["disease_id"]))
382:                         found.append({"type": "Disease",
383:                                       "id": self.disease["disease_id"],
384:                                       "label": self.disease["name"],
385:                                       "matched_text": window})
